## 1. Any Unitary Gate Can Be Certified Device-Independently In A Quantum Network

- **Score**: 9.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-07-15
- **Authors**: Shubhayan Sarkar
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/m1tx-9mx1](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/m1tx-9mx1)
- **Tags**: `device-independent certification`  `self-testing`  `quantum network`  `unitary gate`  `Bell nonlocality`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a device-independent protocol to certify any unitary gate in a quantum network using only observed statistics.

### Motivation

Device-independent certification of quantum gates is crucial for secure quantum networks, but previous self-testing results were limited to specific gates. A general method for arbitrary unitary gates was missing.

### Method

The author constructs a Bell-type scenario involving a quantum network with multiple parties and uses the observed correlations to certify the implementation of an arbitrary unitary gate via a self-testing argument based on the structure of the network.

### Result

The protocol can certify any unitary gate up to a global phase, with robustness bounds that scale linearly with the noise level, making it experimentally feasible.

### Conclusion

This work provides a complete device-independent certification of unitary gates, advancing quantum network security and verification.

*Keywords: device-independent certification, self-testing, quantum network, unitary gate, Bell nonlocality*

## 2. Anomalous Heat Flows And Quantum Otto Engine With (In)definite Causal Order

- **Score**: 9.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-07-14
- **Authors**: Qing-Feng Xue, Qi Zhang, Xu-Cai Zhuang, Ying-Jie Zhang, Yun-Jie Xia, Enrico Russo, Giulio Chiribella, Rosario Lo Franco, And Zhong-Xiao Man
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/sx1m-pdhz](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/sx1m-pdhz)
- **Tags**: `ai`  `order`  `indefinite causal order`  `quantum Otto engine`  `heat flow`

### TL;DR

This paper demonstrates that indefinite causal order can reverse the natural direction of heat flow and enhance the performance of a quantum Otto engine.

### Motivation

Classical thermodynamics dictates heat flows from hot to cold, but quantum effects may challenge this. The authors explore whether indefinite causal order, a quantum feature, can lead to anomalous heat flows and improve engine efficiency.

### Method

They consider a quantum Otto engine where the working substance undergoes two thermalization processes with an indefinite causal order, modeled using a quantum switch. They analyze the heat flow and work output under this setup.

### Result

Indefinite causal order can cause heat to flow from cold to hot, and the engine can achieve higher efficiency and power compared to the definite order case, even exceeding the Carnot bound in certain regimes.

### Conclusion

Indefinite causal order offers a new resource for quantum thermodynamics, enabling anomalous heat flows and enhanced engine performance, with potential implications for quantum thermal machines.

*Keywords: indefinite causal order, quantum Otto engine, heat flow, quantum thermodynamics, quantum switch*

### Conclusion

The aQPU provides a unified model to study the physical resources required for quantum computation, bridging quantum thermodynamics and information processing.

### Conclusion

- **Tags**: `adiabatic state preparation`  `thermal states`  `entropy-noise relation`  `noisy quantum computers`  `quantum thermodynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes an adiabatic protocol to prepare thermal states on noisy quantum computers and derives an entropy-noise relation.

### Motivation

Preparing thermal states is crucial for quantum thermodynamics and many-body physics, but existing methods are often inefficient or require error correction. The authors aim to provide a practical method for near-term noisy devices.

### Method

The authors design an adiabatic state preparation protocol that gradually evolves the system Hamiltonian while coupling to a heat bath, and analyze the trade-off between entropy production and noise using a Lindblad master equation.

### Result

They derive an entropy-noise relation that quantifies how the entropy of the prepared state depends on the noise strength and protocol duration, and demonstrate that thermal states can be prepared with high fidelity under realistic noise conditions.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical framework for preparing thermal states on noisy quantum computers, with implications for quantum thermodynamics and simulation of many-body systems.

*Keywords: adiabatic state preparation, thermal states, entropy-noise relation, noisy quantum computers, quantum thermodynamics*

### TL;DR

Introduces a simplified quantum dynamical entropy that grows linearly in time, with a growth rate bounded by temperature, linking chaos and thermodynamics.

### Motivation

To define a quantum analog of classical dynamical entropy that captures information gain from continuous measurements and connects to thermodynamic bounds.

### Method

Proposes a simplified version of Connes-Narnhofer-Thirring entropy based on monitoring an observable, and derives a bound on its growth rate using the energy-temperature uncertainty relation.

### Result

The entropy growth rate is bounded by a constant times temperature, reminiscent of the Planckian bound on chaos, and is nonzero for chaotic systems.

### Conclusion

- **Published**: 2026-07-16
- **Authors**: James B. Larsen, Tameem Albash, Alicia B. Magann, And Christian Arenz
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/p78c-6p56](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/p78c-6p56)
- **Tags**: `quantum speed limit`  `open quantum systems`  `quantum control`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `Bures angle`

### TL;DR

Derives trajectory-independent quantum speed limits for controlled open quantum systems, providing fundamental bounds on evolution time that depend only on initial and final states.

### Motivation

Existing quantum speed limits for open systems depend on the control trajectory, limiting their predictive power. This work aims to find bounds that are independent of the path taken.

### Method

Uses geometric and information-theoretic arguments, relating the Bures angle between states to the integral of the quantum Fisher information along any trajectory, leading to a trajectory-independent bound.

### Result

Establishes a speed limit that depends only on the initial and final states and the maximum allowed control amplitude, applicable to general Markovian open quantum systems.

### Conclusion

The trajectory-independent speed limit provides a fundamental constraint on the speed of quantum state transformations under control, with implications for quantum control and thermodynamics.

*Keywords: quantum speed limit, open quantum systems, quantum control, quantum thermodynamics, Bures angle*

### TL;DR

This paper investigates the quantum Mpemba effect in the relaxation dynamics of operators in open quantum systems, showing that initial states farther from steady state can relax faster.

### Motivation

The quantum Mpemba effect is a counterintuitive phenomenon where a system farther from equilibrium relaxes faster than one closer. Understanding this effect in open quantum systems can reveal fundamental aspects of quantum thermodynamics and relaxation processes.

### Method

The authors study the relaxation of operators in open quantum systems using a Lindblad master equation approach, analyzing the time evolution of operator expectation values and comparing relaxation rates for different initial conditions.

### Result

They demonstrate the existence of the Mpemba-like effect for operators, where certain initial operator states exhibit accelerated relaxation compared to states initially closer to the steady state.

### Conclusion

The work extends the quantum Mpemba effect to operator dynamics, providing insights into non-equilibrium quantum thermodynamics and relaxation phenomena in open systems.

### TL;DR

This paper examines three definitions of internal energy, work, and heat for strongly coupled quantum systems undergoing sudden quenches, and tests which satisfy the second law.

### Motivation

Strongly coupled quantum systems lack a universally accepted definition of thermodynamic quantities like work and heat, which is essential for understanding quantum thermodynamics.

### Method

The authors analyze three sets of definitions for internal energy, work, and heat in the context of sudden quenches, and test compliance with the first and second laws of thermodynamics using a two-spin model.

### Result

### Conclusion

The study provides guidance on choosing consistent thermodynamic definitions for strongly coupled quantum systems, advancing the foundations of quantum thermodynamics.

## 20. Unified Speed Limits In Classical And Quantum Dynamics Via Temporal Fisher Information

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. E  |  **Feed**: Physical Review E
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-07-14

- **Tags**: `ai`  `speed limit`  `Fisher information`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `nonequilibrium thermodynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper unifies classical and quantum speed limits using temporal Fisher information, establishing a fundamental trade-off between the rate of change of a system and the Fisher information about time.

### Motivation

Speed limits are crucial in thermodynamics and quantum mechanics, but a unified framework connecting classical and quantum versions was missing. The authors aim to derive a general bound using Fisher information.

### Method

They define temporal Fisher information for both classical and quantum dynamics, and prove that the speed of state change is bounded by the square root of the Fisher information. For quantum systems, they use the symmetric logarithmic derivative.

### Result

The unified speed limit holds for arbitrary classical stochastic and quantum unitary dynamics, and reduces to known bounds in specific cases. They also show that the bound is tight for certain systems.

### Conclusion

Temporal Fisher information provides a universal measure of speed in dynamics, bridging classical and quantum speed limits and offering insights into thermodynamic trade-offs.

*Keywords: speed limit, Fisher information, quantum thermodynamics, nonequilibrium thermodynamics, unified dynamics*

- **Tags**: `non-Gaussianity`  `symplectic rank`  `continuous-variable quantum information`  `resource theory`  `bosonic quantum states`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces the symplectic rank as a unified measure of non-Gaussianity for bosonic quantum states, quantifying the minimum resources needed for state preparation, compression, and measurement.

### Motivation

Non-Gaussian states are essential for quantum advantage in continuous-variable systems, but a unified measure of non-Gaussianity was lacking. The symplectic rank fills this gap by providing an operationally meaningful resource measure.

### Method

The authors define the symplectic rank based on the minimal number of Gaussian unitaries required to prepare a state from a Gaussian state, and they develop tools to compute it for various classes of states, including Fock states, cat states, and Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill states.

### Result

The symplectic rank is shown to be additive under tensor products, monotonic under Gaussian operations, and to provide tight lower bounds on the number of non-Gaussian operations needed for state preparation and compression.

### Conclusion

The symplectic rank is a powerful and practical measure of non-Gaussianity with clear operational interpretations, advancing the resource theory of non-Gaussianity in continuous-variable quantum information.

*Keywords: non-Gaussianity, symplectic rank, continuous-variable quantum information, resource theory, bosonic quantum states*

### TL;DR

This paper presents a three-qubit quantum error-correcting code tailored for amplitude-damping noise and extends it to a family of codes correcting arbitrary orders of damping.

### Motivation

Amplitude-damping noise is a common decoherence mechanism in quantum systems, yet most quantum error-correcting codes are designed for generic errors. There is a need for noise-adapted codes that are more efficient for specific noise models.

### Method

The authors construct a three-qubit code that corrects all single-qubit amplitude-damping errors using a probabilistic quantum error correction framework with postselected recovery. They generalize this to a family of codes that correct up to any fixed order in the damping strength and derive a noise-adapted quantum Hamming bound.

### Result

The three-qubit code is the smallest possible for correcting single-qubit amplitude-damping errors, and the family of codes achieves higher rates than generic codes for this noise model.

### Conclusion

Noise-adapted quantum error correction can significantly reduce resource overhead for amplitude-damping noise, and the probabilistic framework provides a systematic way to construct such codes.

*Keywords: quantum error correction, amplitude-damping noise, noise-adapted codes, probabilistic error correction, quantum Hamming bound*

### TL;DR

This paper provides the first rigorous proof that quantum Wiener systems, consisting of linear quantum dynamics with weak measurements and classical nonlinear readouts, can approximate any fading memory nonlinear system, establishing a theoretical foundation for quantum reservoir computing.

### Motivation

Quantum reservoir computing (QRC) is a promising paradigm for temporal information processing, but lacks rigorous theoretical guarantees. The authors aim to provide a solid mathematical foundation for QRC using the Wiener architecture.

### Method

The authors define quantum Wiener systems as linear quantum networks with weak continuous measurements and classical nonlinear readouts. They prove that such systems are universal approximators for fading memory nonlinear systems using the theory of Volterra series and the Stone-Weierstrass theorem.

### Result

They show that any fading memory nonlinear system can be approximated arbitrarily well by a quantum Wiener system, and provide explicit bounds on the approximation error in terms of the number of quantum nodes and measurement strength.

### Conclusion

Quantum Wiener systems offer a theoretically grounded and practically viable approach to quantum reservoir computing, with potential advantages in computational power and energy efficiency over classical reservoirs.

The new formalism predicts a glass transition temperature and reproduces key features of glassy dynamics, such as a Kauzmann-like entropy crisis.

### Method

### Result

### Conclusion

- **Tags**: `ai`  `physics`  `control`  `surface`  `experiment`

### TL;DR

This paper reports the first experimental observation of Floquet tensor monopoles in a four-dimensional non-equilibrium system, demonstrating their creation, motion, and annihilation controlled by driving frequency.

### Motivation

Tensor monopoles have been studied in static settings, but their non-equilibrium counterparts (Floquet tensor monopoles) and associated anomalous topological features had not been realized.

### Method

The authors design and fabricate time-varying topological circuits to realize a four-dimensional non-equilibrium system, and observe the controllable motion of Floquet tensor monopoles in momentum space and their induced 3D Fermi-arc surface states by tuning the driving frequency.

### Result

They observe driving-frequency controlled creation, motion, and annihilation of Floquet tensor monopoles, along with anomalous bulk-edge correspondence unique to the non-equilibrium regime.

### Conclusion

This work extends tensor monopole research to non-equilibrium systems, providing a platform for exploring exotic topological phases in dynamic tensor gauge fields and functional topological devices with real-time control.

*Keywords: Floquet tensor monopoles, non-equilibrium topological phases, tensor gauge fields, topological circuits, Fermi-arc surface states*

This paper establishes a mathematical correspondence between Laplace and Helmholtz equations to design metamaterials for wave manipulation using static diffusion fields.

### Motivation

To bridge the gap between static and dynamic physics, enabling complex wave functionalities (e.g., hyperbolic invisibility, 3D cloaking) through simpler steady-state design.

### Method

The authors derive a wave invariant that maps the single-parameter Laplace equation to the two-parameter Helmholtz equation, allowing wave parameters and fields to be obtained from static solutions.

### Result

They demonstrate hyperbolic invisibility and 3D freeform conformal cloaking with broadband performance, and experimentally validate a waveguide cloak.

### Conclusion

The Laplace-Helmholtz correspondence provides a versatile gateway for manipulating waves beyond conventional constraints, enabling a 'less-for-more' paradigm in metamaterial design.

### TL;DR

This paper reports the coexistence of high-temperature superconductivity and antiferromagnetic order in a cuprate with multiple hole Fermi pockets.

### Motivation

Understanding the interplay between superconductivity and antiferromagnetism in cuprates is a long-standing problem in condensed matter physics.

### Method

The authors synthesized Bi2Sr2Ca6Cu7O18+δ single crystals with seven CuO2 planes and performed angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) to map the electronic structure.

### Result

They observed multiple hole-like Fermi pockets in the nodal region and strong electron pairing with a gap up to ~42 meV on an underdoped inner plane that exhibits robust antiferromagnetic order.

### Conclusion

The coexistence of high-temperature superconductivity and antiferromagnetic order in the same CuO2 plane challenges conventional understanding and suggests a more complex relationship between these two phenomena.

### Conclusion

The drift configuration dependence of the radial electric field is a key factor in determining H-mode access, providing a theoretical basis for optimizing tokamak operation.

### Motivation

To test the cosmological principle by comparing the dipole anisotropy in the distribution of radio galaxies and quasars with the cosmic microwave background dipole.

Analysis of recent large catalogs of radio galaxies and quasars to measure the dipole in the matter distribution and compare it with the CMB dipole.

### Result

A significant inconsistency is found between the two dipoles, suggesting a possible violation of the cosmological principle.

### Conclusion

The cosmic dipole anomaly may challenge the standard ΛCDM model, but further investigation of biases and systematics is needed.

- **Tags**: `order`  `photon`  `interference`  `dynamics`  `photoionization`

### TL;DR

This paper studies parity-mixing interference in laser-assisted photoionization of helium, a topic in atomic physics and ultrafast optics.

### Motivation

To investigate quantum interference pathways that violate parity conservation in photoionization, which can reveal information about photoionization dynamics or light fields.

### Method

High-order harmonics generated by a few-cycle laser are used to ionize helium, and three-dimensional electron detection is employed to identify interference pathways.

### Result

Four parity-mixing interference pathways are identified, involving one- and two-photon transitions with absorption or emission of a probe photon.

### Conclusion

Parity-mixing interference provides a new tool for studying photoionization dynamics and characterizing light fields.

*Keywords: photoionization, high-order harmonics, parity-mixing interference, laser-assisted, helium*

- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-07-13
- **Authors**: Beomsoo Chun, Byong Jae Kim, Hyeongjin Kim, Jeong Han Song, Yeongsik Kim, Jaehoon Lim And Jeonghun Kwak
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8470](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8470)
- **Tags**: `ai`  `control`  `scattering`  `quantum-dot`  `microscopy`

### TL;DR

This paper uses machine learning to optimize solvent mixtures for uniform quantum dot packing in QLEDs, achieving high efficiency and long lifetime.

### Motivation

Achieving uniformly packed emissive layers is a bottleneck for high-performance quantum-dot light-emitting diodes (QLEDs).

### Method

Five solvent parameters were evaluated, and multiple regression models (with support vector regression best) were trained against film uniformity from AFM; a mixed solvent was formulated based on predictions.

### Result

QLEDs with the optimized solvent exhibited 20.6% external quantum efficiency and 468.5 h lifetime at 20,000 cd/m², outperforming single-solvent controls.

### Conclusion

Packing homogeneity is a decisive factor for device performance, and the data-driven framework is generalizable for solution-processed devices.

*Keywords: quantum dot light-emitting diodes, machine learning, solvent engineering, film uniformity, support vector regression*

- **Authors**: Sujit Rajak, Nishkarsh Kumar, Dheeraj Yadav, Suman Mandal, Jeeban K Nayak, Ayan Banerjee, Subhasish Dutta Gupta, Olivier Martin And Nirmalya Ghosh
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8695](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae8695)
- **Tags**: `ai`  `spin`  `control`  `surface`  `metasurface`

### TL;DR

This paper reports a Rashba-like spin splitting effect in an inversion-symmetric plasmonic metasurface, mediated by geometric phase gradients.

### Motivation

To demonstrate spin-split dispersion in systems with inversion symmetry, which is typically forbidden, and to explore new paradigms for spin-based nano-optics.

### Method

Momentum domain polarization analysis is used to identify a transverse momentum-dependent linear birefringence–linear dichroism effect, and the geometric phase gradient is shown to mediate the spin-split dispersion.

### Result

An extraordinary spin-split dispersion effect of leaky surface plasmons is observed in an inversion-symmetric plasmonic crystal, arising from geometric LB–LD effects.

### Conclusion

This unconventional spin-orbit interaction phenomenon enables spin-based dispersion engineering and spin-controlled nano-optical functionalities in symmetric metastructures.

*Keywords: Rashba effect, spin-orbit interaction, plasmonic metasurface, geometric phase, spin splitting*

### Motivation

Most projections of Antarctic ice loss omit fracture mechanics, which may significantly accelerate ice flow.

Including fracture mechanics is crucial for accurate sea-level rise projections.
